## *THE PREFACE.*

It is one of the most commendable <term>indulgencies: permissions or kind allowances granted by an authority</term> of our <term>Church: the Church of England</term>, that she allows us a <note>original: "latitude." This refers to a wide scope or freedom of thought.</note> freedom of judging in points of *Theoretical Debate* <note>original: "Speculation." In this context, it refers to theological or philosophical questions that are not explicitly settled by scripture or law.</note>. And she does not tie up the consciences of men to a blind <note>original: "implicit." This means accepting something without personal investigation or proof.</note> agreement with opinions that are not necessary or *Foundational* <note>original: "Fundamental." These are the core doctrines of the faith that are considered essential for salvation.</note>; which favorable and kind <term>permission: the formal allowance to think or act freely</term> is undoubtedly a great obligation upon the <term>ingenious: people of intellect, refinement, and high character</term> to submissively receive and observe her religious <term>appointments: official decrees, rituals, or regulations</term> for peace and order. Nor is there less *Reason* in this fatherly kindness <note>original: "parental indulgence." The author views the Church's authority as a gentle parent rather than a harsh tyrant.</note>, than there is of <term>Christian charity: the theological virtue of selfless, divine love for others</term>.

<summary>The author opens his preface by praising the Church of England for allowing intellectual freedom on non-essential theological issues, arguing that such kindness encourages order and loyalty among its members.</summary>
<keywords>The Preface, Church of England, religious freedom, speculation, fundamentalism, Christian charity, 17th-century theology</keywords>

He contrasts the Church of England’s moderate "appointments" with the strict, newly-invented "orthodoxies" of its critics, which he labels as a form of tyranny.</meta>

## C H A P. I.

### *The opinions proposed concerning the origin of Souls.*

IT has always been found a matter of discouraging difficulty, among those that have busied themselves in such *Inquiries*, to determine the *Soul's origin.* So much so that after all the contests and disputes that have been about it, many of the wisest seekers <note>original: "Inquisitors"</note> have concluded it *undeterminable*; or, if they have settled on either of the two opinions, *namely* <note>original: "viz."</note> of its *immediate Creation* <note>the theological theory that God creates a new soul specifically for every new body</note>, or *Traduc-* <term>Traduction: the theory that the soul is generated from the parents (like the body) rather than being a separate act of creation by God</term>

<summary>This page introduces the primary subject of the book: the difficult and debated question of where the human soul originates. The author notes that many scholars find the matter impossible to settle, typically choosing between the theories of immediate divine creation or biological inheritance.</summary>

<keywords>origin of the soul, Lux Orientalis, creationism, traduction, philosophy, theology</keywords>

And indeed, if we do but impartially consider the grand inconveniences which each party urgeth against the others Conclusion, it would even tempt one to think, that both are right in their *opposition* and neither in their *assertion*. Wherefore if there be a *third* that can lay any probable claim to the truth, it deserves to be heard to plead its cause; and, if it be not chargeable with the contradictions or absurdities either of the one or other, to be admitted.

## CHAPTER II.

### *The daily creation of Souls is inconsistent with the Divine Attributes.*

The first of these opinions that offers itself to be put to the test <note>original: "Tryal"</note> is, *that God daily creates human souls, which are immediately joined to the bodies that reproduction* <term>Generation: the biological process of procreation</term> *has prepared for them.* On this side of the debate are our modern <term>Divines: theologians or clergy members</term>, and the majority of the <term>Schoolmen: medieval philosophers who used systematic logic to study Christian doctrine</term>. But so as not to be overwhelmed <note>original: "born down"</note> by the weight of these authorities, let us consider what reason stands against it. Therefore,

(1) If our souls came directly out of the hands of God when we first entered these bodies, then where do those enormously animal-like <note>original: "brutish"</note> inclinations, that strong natural tendency toward vice and godlessness, that are present in the children of men come from? All the works of God reflect his image, and are perfect in their kind. Purity is his nature, and what

### Praexistence of Souls,

But others think to evade the issue by saying that we do not have these depravities <term>depravities: moral corruptions or sinful tendencies</term> in our natures, but rather contract them by *custom*, *education*, and *evil practices*. How then does it happen that those who have had the same care and industry used upon them, and have been nurtured under the same discipline and severe oversight, differ so vastly and even to a wondrous degree in their *inclinations*? How is it that those who are under continual temptations toward *vice* are yet kept within the bounds of *virtue* and sobriety? Surely, there is something more to the matter than those general causes, which may be common to both, and which many times have quite contrary effects.

as *indifferent* <note>original: "indifferent" — meaning neutral; neither naturally leaning toward good nor toward evil.</note> naturally to good and evil as can be supposed; yet great are the disadvantages to *virtue* that all men unavoidably meet with, in this state of *imperfection*.

For considering that our *infant* and *growing* age is an age of *sense* <note>A period of life governed by physical feelings and immediate needs rather than logic or morality.</note>, in which our *appetites* and *passions* are very strong, and our *reasons* weak, and scarce anything but a chain of *imaginations* <note>The author suggests that a child's mind is a collection of fleeting mental images and impulses rather than structured rational thought.</note>, it is—I say—great odds but that we should be carried to inordinacy <term>inordinacy: a lack of restraint or a tendency to exceed proper moral bounds.</term>, and exceed the bounds the divine laws have set us. So that our *lower powers of sense and passions* using to have the head <note>Meaning these animalistic impulses are in control or "have a head start" before reason develops.</note>, will grow strong and impetuous; and thus it is an hundred to one but we shall be rooted in *vice*, before we come to the *maturity* of our *reasons*, or are capable of the exercise of *virtue*.

## CHAPTER III.

### (2) Transmission <term>Traduction</term> <note>original: "Traduction"; the theory that the soul is passed from parents to offspring during reproduction, similar to physical traits.</note> of souls is impossible, the reasons for it are weak and frivolous, and the proposal of Pre-existence <note>original: "Præexis-tence"; the doctrine that the soul exists in a spiritual realm before entering a physical body.</note>.

Thus then we have examined the first way of stating the *Soul’s origin*, that of *continual Creation*; and finding no sure resting place for our inquiry here, we move to the second.

The way of *Transmission* <term>Traduction</term> or *biological propagation* <note>original: "seminal Propagation"</note>. And the followers of this *theory* <note>original: "Hypothesis"</note> are of two sorts, namely <note>original: "viz."</note>, either those who make the soul to be nothing but a *purer sort of matter*, or those who admit it is wholly *spiritual and immaterial*. I will dispatch the former group quickly, strike at the root of their misconception regarding the *soul’s production*, and show that it cannot be *Matter*, be it as pure as can be imagined.

### A Key for Providence.

with principles logical, moral, and metaphysical <note>metaphysical: relating to the fundamental nature of reality and the soul, beyond what can be seen or measured.</note>, which could never owe their origin <note>original: "original"</note> to *sense* in any other way than that the senses give us the opportunity to use them. For *sense* teaches no *general propositions* <note>Universal truths, such as "the whole is greater than the part."</note>, but only provides *singulars* <note>individual, specific examples encountered through the eyes or ears.</note> for <term>Induction<gloss>The logical process of forming a general rule after observing specific examples.</gloss></term>; which, being an *Inference*, must proceed from a higher *principle* that has no such dependence on the *senses*, because it is found within the mind and not derived from anything outside of it. ->A Key for Providence.<-

it seems to me that if we possess such a vast power as to bring the ends of contradictions together—making something out of nothing (which some deny even to God Almighty <note>original: "Omnipotence it self"</note>)—it is very strange that we cannot *preserve* <gloss>conserve</gloss> the existence of the creature we have so *produced*, nor even our own internal selves, especially since *preservation* is no greater a feat than *creation*. And it is strange that in other matters we should show so few *examples* <gloss>specimens</gloss> of such a vast *ability*; or that we should have a power so *divine* and *excellent*, yet have no mental faculty to recognize it.

Again (2) if the *Soul* is *immediately* produced out of nothing, regardless of who the agent is—whether *God* or the *Parent*—it would be pure and *sinless*. For, even if we suppose our parents to be our *Creators*, they produce us only as *natural causes* <gloss>natural agents</gloss>, and so they can only transmit their *natural* <note>physical or biological</note> qualities, but not their *moral wickedness* <term>moral pravities<gloss>The theological concept of inherent sin or moral corruption believed to be inherited from Adam.</gloss></term>. Therefore, this viewpoint provides no better account of how the soul is so debauched and infected as soon as it enters the body than the previous theory did, and it therefore fails in the main purpose for which it was intended.

Thus we see then that the *transmission* <term>traduction<gloss>The "traduction" theory suggests that the soul is generated by the parents during procreation, much like the body, rather than being a new creation by God.</gloss></term>

### CHAPTER IV.

(1) *Preexistence <note>The doctrine that the soul existed before the body was created.</note> cannot be disproved. Scripture says nothing against it. Its silence is no prejudice <gloss>detriment or objection</gloss> to this Doctrine, but rather an Argument for it, as the case stands. Preexistence was the common opinion of our Savior's times. How, probably, it came to be lost in the Christian Church.*

Therefore let us see what claim <gloss>title</gloss> it can show for our agreement <gloss>assent</gloss>, or whether it can prove itself worthy of the *support* <gloss>Patronage</gloss> of those great *Authors* that have embraced <gloss>owned</gloss> it.

If those who assert the doctrine of **daily Creation** <note>the theological belief that God creates a new soul for every individual at the moment of conception, rather than souls existing beforehand</note> think it is clear from Scripture that **God** is the **Father of Spirits**, and the **immediate maker of souls**, they will find that this same point is confirmed and supported by this **Hypothesis** <note>the theory of the Pre-existence of souls</note>. It provides a reasonable account of how we all **die** in **Adam** without casting a stain upon the **Wisdom**, **Justice**, or **Goodness** of **God**, and without claiming anything that contradicts the **Appearances of Nature**.

Therefore, (2) it may be that some are so careless as to argue against our souls having existed from of old because **Sacred Scripture** <note>original: "Sacred writ"</note> says, "**We are but of Yesterday**" <note>A reference to Job 8:9: "For we are but of yesterday, and know nothing, because our days upon earth are a shadow."</note>. This expression in the divine **Scripture** is without question to be understood as referring to our **appearance** on this stage of **Earth**. It is no more an **Argument** against our **Pre-existence** than that other phrase of his, "**Before I go hence, and Be no more**" <note>A reference to Psalm 39:13: "O spare me, that I may recover strength, before I go hence, and be no more."</note>, is an argument against our future existence in another state after this present life has ended. Nor will it prove the point for which it is brought any more than the expression of **Rachel** weeping for her **Children because they were not** <note>Referencing Jeremiah 31:15 or Matthew 2:18; a poetic way of saying the children had died, not that they never existed or were reduced to nothingness</note> would imply that they were **absolutely** nothing.

-># A Key for Providence.<-

...better is he than both they (meaning the dead and the living) who has not yet been <note>A reference to Ecclesiastes 4:3: "Yea, better is he than both they, which hath not yet been, who hath not seen the evil work that is done under the sun."</note>. Since this is a similar figure of speech to the one mentioned before, it seems to support rather than discourage the idea of **Pre-existence** <note>original: "Præexistence"; the theory that the soul exists before the body is conceived</note>. This is because something that is absolutely nothing can be neither **worse** nor **better**.

Moreover, when we come from a state of silence and inactivity and drop into these **bodies**, we were previously as if we **had not been**. Therefore, there is a better reason in this case for using such a manner of speaking than there is in the case of a mere **non-appearance**; yet the **Scripture** phrases this as **"not being."**

And now I cannot think of any other passage in the **Sacred Volume** <note>The Bible</note> that could make a plausible argument against this **Hypothesis** <note>original: "Hypothesis"; from the Greek *hupothesis*, meaning a foundational proposition or theory</note>—the idea that our souls existed before they entered these **bodies**—unless:

# A Key for Providence.

(2) Why is it considered so absurd that the soul should have *animated* <note>original: "actuated"</note> another kind of body before it entered *this* one? Even in our current state, the soul is directly joined to a purer *vehicle*, and through it, moves and governs the heavier physical body. Why then could the soul not have been joined solely to such a refined body in its former and purer state of life—a body that would have been suited to its own *perfection* and *purity*? I am certain that many, if not most, of the *Ancient Fathers* <note>The influential bishops and theologians of the early Christian Church.</note> believed that *angels* themselves were *embodied*, and therefore they did not regard *this* idea as such a gross absurdity. However, a later opportunity will lead us to write on this subject again, so I will move on to a third response to this *objection*.

(3) Therefore, even if it were granted that the *soul* lived in former times without a *body*, what greater *inconsistency* <note>original: "incongruity"</note> is there in such a *supposition* than the idea that the soul should live and *act* after death without any union with *matter* or any body whatsoever? Yet, this is exactly what the *objectors* themselves believe happens. All such objections as these will vanish like mists before the sun,

when we shall come particularly to state the *Hypothesis* <note>The "Hypothesis" refers to the specific theory or logical model the author is proposing.</note>. Especially since, as hath been intimated, they prove nothing at all, but that the objectors cannot conceive what manner of state that of *Pre-existence* was, which is no prejudice <note>In this context, "prejudice" refers to an intellectual obstacle or a preconceived bias that hinders one's judgment.</note> to the opinion itself; that *our souls were extant before these earthly bodies*.

Thus then I hope I have clearly enough made good *that all souls might have been created from the beginning*; for ought any thing that is made known, either in the *Scriptures* or our *reasons* to the contrary. ### *A second Argument for Pre-existence drawn from the consideration of the Divine Goodness, which always does what is best.*

(2) Then, whoever conceives rightly of *God*, understands him to be infinite and immense *Goodness*, who is always pouring out his own *overflowing fullness*: There is no *narrowness* <note>The original text uses "straightness" (straitness), an archaic term for tightness or stinginess, suggesting God has no limitations in his generosity.</note> in the *Deity*, and no bounds to the ocean of *Love*. 

Now, the divine *Goodness* does not refer to himself, just as our own goodness does not extend to him <note>This follows the theological principle that God is self-sufficient; he does not act out of a need to improve himself or gain anything, as he is already perfect.</note>. Now, just as the *infinite Goodness* of the deity obliges him always to do *good*, so by the same logic it obliges him to do that which is *best*; since to omit any degree of *good* would suggest a defect in *goodness*, assuming *wisdom* is there to plan it and *power* to carry it out.

He therefore that supposes *God* does not always do what is *best*, and *best* for his *Creatures* (for he cannot act for his own *benefit* <note>original: "Good"</note>), perceives him to be less good than can be imagined, and consequently not *infinitely* good. (2) The *divine liberty* and *freedome* consists not in his acting by meer *arbitrarious will* <note>The author is arguing against the idea that God acts randomly or without reason; "arbitrarious" here means acting solely by whim.</note> as *disjunct* from his other *Attributes*. (3) It is a belittling of his *infinite Majesty* to claim anything that goes against his *Goodness* under the excuse of his *will* and *pleasure*. For whatever is *most fitting* to this most blessed *Attribute* <term>Attribute: In theology, a fundamental quality or characteristic of God's nature, such as goodness, wisdom, or power.</term>, and does not contradict any other, *we can be certain that is what he wills*. Therefore:

(4) If it would be better and more *consistent* with the *divine goodness* for us to have existed in a *happier state* before we entered these *bodies*, *God's will* cannot then be used as an argument to the contrary (especially since it has already been proven that he has never *revealed* any such will of his). Instead, it is *demonstrably* clear that his *will was that it should be so*. Since just as God never acts without his *wisdom* and *goodness*, he also never refrains from acting when those great *Attributes* require it.

It is true that this *Hypothesis* <term>Hypothesis: A theory or proposed explanation; here, the belief that souls exist in a spiritual state before being born into human bodies.</term> is most suitable to the *divine goodness*, and the consideration of that alone would lead us to conclude it. I reply briefly: that if it is admitted to be so consistent with, and derivable from one *Attribute* <term>Attribute: A fundamental quality or characteristic of God’s nature, such as goodness, wisdom, or power.</term>, and cannot be proved *inconsistent* with another, my argument is settled. Therefore, let those who claim an inconsistency prove it. 

(2) The *wisdom of God* is that Attribute and *essential perfection* by which the *divine actions* are directed to their *end*, which is always *good* and *best*. Therefore, to do that which is *best* cannot go against the *divine wisdom*, but always includes and assumes it. From this it follows that whatever aligns with *goodness* cannot stand opposite to *Wisdom*. *Wisdom* in God is indeed nothing else but *goodness* planning and directing for the creature's *good* and happiness. For we must remember what was said above: that what *is infinitely full and perfect can have no goals for any self-advantage* <note>The author argues that because God is already complete and perfect, He does not act to gain anything for Himself; His only "motive" is the benefit of His creatures.</note>; and

<margin>1</margin> It is pointless for those learned men to argue whether a state of the most extreme *misery* a creature is capable of—and an *everlasting* one at that—is not better than *non-existence* <note>original: "Non-entity", the state of not existing at all.</note>.

(2) If we cannot know for certain that it would have been better for us to have existed in a life of happiness, suited to our natures in the distant past, rather than having been mere nothingness until only a few years ago; then we could never recognize or acknowledge the divine goodness toward us in anything we enjoy. For if it might have been just as good for us *not to Be*, as *to Be* and to be happy; then it might have been just as good for us to have lacked anything else that we enjoy as it is to have it. Consequently, we cannot recognize it as an effect of God's *goodness* that he has bestowed any blessing upon us. For if *Existence* <note>original: "Being"</note> is not better than *Non-existence* <note>original: "Not-being"</note>, then it is no effect of goodness that we *are*; and if that is the case, then it is not out of goodness that we have anything else, since all other things are inferior to the good of *Existence*. # Præexistence of Souls,

Therefore, in summary (fifthly): The *divine goodness* is manifested in making all creatures suitably to those *Ideas* of their natures <note>The author refers to the Platonic concept of "Ideas" or "Forms"—the eternal, perfect blueprints for all things that exist in the mind of God.</note>, which he has in his *All-comprehensive Wisdom*. And their good and *happiness* consists in acting according to those *natures*, and in being furnished with all things necessary for such *actions*. Now, the *divine Wisdom* is no *arbitrary thing* that can change or alter those settled, immutable ideas of things that are there represented. It does not lop off *essential attributes* <note>Essential attributes are the fundamental characteristics that define what a thing is; without them, the creature would cease to be itself.</note> of some *beings* to graft them upon others <note>The original uses the word "inoculate," a gardening term for grafting a bud from one plant onto another.</note>: But, distinctly comprehending all things, assigns each *Being* its proper *nature* and *qualities*. And the *Divine goodness*, according to the wise direction of the *eternal intellect*, in like distinct and orderly manner produces all things: *namely* <note>original: "viz."</note>, according to all the variety of their respective *ideas* in the divine wisdom. *Great caution to be used in alleging Scripture for our speculative opinions. The support that Pre-existence receives from the sacred writings of both the Old and New Testaments; Reasons for the apparent strangeness of these claims. Pre-existence stood in no need of proof from Scripture.*

It will be next expected, that I should now prove the doctrine I have undertaken to defend by Scripture evidence, and make good what I said above: That the *divine oracles* <note>A common 17th-century term for the Bible or Holy Scripture.</note> are not so silent in this matter as is imagined. But truly I have so tender a regard for the sacred Authority of that *Holy volume*, that I dare not be so bold with it as to force it to speak what I think it does not intend; a *presumption* <note>In this context, "presumption" refers to the intellectual arrogance of overstepping one's bounds.</note> that is too common among our confident *opinionists* <term>Opinionists</term> <note>A term used in the 1600s for people who dogmatically held onto their own private theories or sect-based beliefs.</note>, and that has caused great troubles to the *Church*, and

### Præexistence of Soul

Now however *uncouth* <note>In this 17th-century context, "uncouth" means strange, unusual, or unfamiliar.</note> these *allegations* <note>Citations or arguments brought forward as proof.</note> may seem to those that never heard these Scriptures thus interpreted; yet I am confident, had the opinion of *Preexistence* <term>Preexistence<note>The belief that the human soul exists in a spiritual state before being united with a physical body at birth.</note></term> been a received Doctrine, and had these Texts been wont to be applied to the proof of it <note>original: "on't"</note>, they would then have been thought to assert it, with clear and convictive *evidence*. 

But many having never heard of this *Hypothesis*, and those that have, seldom meeting it mentioned but as a *silly dream* or *antiquated absurdity*, 'tis no wonder that they never suspect it to be lodged in the *Sacred volume* <note>The Holy Bible.</note>, so that any attempt to confirm it from there, must needs seem rather an *offer of wit* <note>A display of cleverness or intellectual maneuvering rather than a sincere search for truth.</note> than *serious judgment*. 

And the places that are cited to that purpose having been frequently read and heard of, by those that never discerned them to breathe the least air of any such matter as *Preexistence*, their *new* and *unexpected application* to a thing so *little* thought of, must needs seem a *wild fetch* <note>A far-fetched or extravagant stretch of the imagination.</note> of an extravagant imagination. I find myself obliged to go a little further than the bare proof and defense of **Preexistence** <term>Preexistence<note>The philosophical theory that the human soul existed in a spiritual form before being born into a physical body.</note></term>. For though what I have said may possibly induce some to think favorably of our conclusion—that *the souls of men were made before they came into these bodies*—yet while they shall think that nothing can be conceived of that former state, and that our **Preexistent** condition cannot be represented to **human understanding** but as a dark, black solitude <note>A state of empty, lonely nothingness that lacks any identifiable activity or purpose.</note>, it must needs weaken the persuasion of those that are less confirmed, and fill the minds of the inquisitive with a dubious trouble and anxiety. For searching and contemplative heads cannot be satisfied to be told that *our souls have lived and acted in a former condition*, except they can be helped to some more particular apprehension of that state: how we lived and acted of old, and how probably we fell from that better life into this **region of misery and imperfection** <note>The physical world and our current human lives, viewed here as a lower state of being compared to the soul's original home.</note>.

Now though indeed my charity would prompt me to do what I can for the re-

## The Fourth Pillar.

( 4 ) *The Souls of men are capable of living in other bodies besides Earthly ones; And never Act but in some body or other.*

For 1. when I consider how deeply in this state we are immersed in the body, I can, it seems to me, scarce imagine that presently upon leaving it, we shall be stripped of all **corporeity** <note>The state of having a physical or material body.</note>, for this would be such a **jump** as is seldom or never made in **nature**; since by almost all instances that come under our observation it is manifest, that she is accustomed to act by due and orderly gradations, and takes no **precipitous** leaps from one extreme to another. ## The Fifth Pillar.

> (5) *The soul in every state has such a body, as is fittest for those faculties and operations that it is most inclined to exercise.*

It is a known Maxim <note>A fundamental truth or principle, specifically the scholastic rule: "Everything that exists, exists for the sake of its function."</note>, *That every thing that is, exists for its operation*; and the designer and maker of the world has been so bountiful to all beings, as to furnish them with all suitable and necessary requirements for their respective actions; for there are no inclinations or dispositions in nature, but some way or other they are brought into *actual* exercise, otherwise they would be mere nullities <note>Non-entities; things that have no reality or purpose.</note>, and irrelevant additions <note>The original uses "impertinent appendices," meaning parts that do not belong or serve no function.</note>. # The Seventh Pillar.

(7) *By the same degrees that the higher powers are invigorated, the lower are lulled to sleep <note>original: consopited; from the Latin 'consopire', meaning to quiet or put to rest.</note> and abated, as to their proper exercises, and vice versa <note>original: & è contra</note>.*

For the *Soul* is a finite and limited being, and therefore cannot operate in various ways with equal intensity at once. That is, it cannot at the same time employ all her faculties in the highest degree of exercise that each of them is capable of. ## CHAP. XIV.

### *A Philosophical Hypothesis of the Soul's <term>pre-existence: the doctrine that the human soul existed in a celestial state before being born into a physical body</term>.*

### The Airy State

In this condition, our bodies are more or less pure, in proportion to the degree of our *falling away* <note>original: "apostacy"</note>. At this stage, there may still be significant remains of *virtue* and *divine love*, even though the *lower life*—that of the physical body—has grown very *strong* and *unrestrained* <note>original: "rampant"</note>. ## The next step of Descent, or After State.

To give an account of the *After State* <note>The condition or existence of the soul after it leaves the physical body.</note> of the more *degenerate* <note>Those whose souls have decayed morally or spiritually.</note> and yet *descending souls*, some imagine a very odd *Hypothesis*, proposing that they pass from here into some other more coarse and inferior *Planet*, in which they are provided with *bodies* suitable to their so depraved natures; But I shall be thought extravagant <note>Exceeding the bounds of reason or common sense.</note> for even mentioning such a supposition; therefore, I move on to what is less *obnoxious* <note>In this context, "obnoxious" means likely to be criticized or objected to.</note>.

When our souls go out of these bodies, they are not immediately discharged of all the matter that belonged to this condition, but carry away their inward and *aerial state* <note>A subtle, spirit-like substance that serves as the soul's "vehicle" after death.</note> to be partakers with them of their *after fortunes*; only leaving the useless earth <note>The heavy, physical body.</note> behind them. For they have a congruity <term>Congruity: From the Latin 'congruitas,' meaning a natural suitability or "fit" between the soul and its specific type of body (physical, airy, or ethereal).</term> to their *airy bodies*, even though the affinity they had for a *terrestrial* one is worn out and defaced.

But now the case is altered; these *lower powers are worn and wearied out* by the tiresome exercise of dragging about and managing such a *load of flesh* <note>A reference to the heavy, physical human body.</note>. Wherefore, being so disciplined, they are duly adjusted to the more easy body of air again, as was mentioned before; to which they being already *united*, they cannot fail to find a proper habitation.

But considering the *stupor, dullness, and inactivity* of our *declining age* <note>The period of old age and physical decay.</note>, it may seem unlikely to some that after death we should immediately be resuscitated <note>Restored to life or vigor.</note> into so *lively and vigorous a condition* as is the *aerial* state. However, notwithstanding this *inability* to manage a *sluggish, stubborn*, and *exhausted terrestrial body* <note>The physical human body made of earth-like matter.</note>, there is no doubt but the *Soul* can with great ease, when it is discharged of its former load, actuate its *thin airy vehicle* <term>Airy vehicle: A subtle body made of air or a spirit-like substance that the soul was thought to inhabit after its departure from the physical body.</term>; and that with a brisk *vigor* and *activity*. As a man that is overladen may be ready to faint and sink until he be relieved of his burden; and *then*, he can run away with a *cheerful vivacity*. So that this *decrepit condition* of our *decayed natures* cannot justly prejudice our belief that we shall be raised *again* into a state of *life* and *action* in *aerial bodies*, after this *congruity* <term>Congruity: The "vital congruity" is a technical term for the soul's natural fitness or capacity to unite with a specific type of body (physical or aerial).</term> is expired.

it is filled with some fluid substances or others, and it may be a kind of *air, fire,* and *water*. Now this *hypothesis* <term>Hypothesis: A proposed explanation made on the basis of limited evidence as a starting point for further investigation.</term> will help us easily to imagine how the *earth* may *move* <note>The author is likely referring to the Earth's rotation and its orbit around the sun, a concept that was still being reconciled with traditional physics in the 17th century.</note>, despite the supposed lack of fitness for motion due to its massive size; and for that reason, I believe it will be somewhat more acceptable to those who are open-minded and clever.

Those who understand *Cartesian philosophy* <term>Cartesian Philosophy: The system of René Descartes, who argued that the universe was a "plenum" (filled with matter) and that the Earth and planets were carried along by vortices of fluid matter.</term> will readily accept this *hypothesis*, or at least as much of it as I shall need. But for others, I have little hope of persuading them of anything, and therefore I will save my effort rather than try to prove what they are either incapable of understanding or would immediately judge to be ridiculous. And perhaps *most* people will grant as much as is required for my purpose, which is: *That there are huge, vast cavities within the body of the earth;* and it would be as unnecessary as it would be arrogant for me to attempt to define anything further. I shall only mention the probability that this *thick crust* which we call *earth* is not of such a great depth as is generally supposed, and so I will come more directly to my business.

Now those miserable prisoners cannot escape from the places of their confinement; for it is very likely that those watchful *spirits* that were instrumental in committing them to prison have a strict and careful eye upon them to keep them within the confines of their jail <note>original: "goal," an archaic spelling of gaol or jail.</note>, so that they do not wander out into the *Regions* of *light* and *liberty*. This may be the reason for the infrequency of their appearance, and why they most commonly retreat at the approach of *light*. Besides all this, some there are who suppose that there is a kind of *government* <note>original: "polity"</note> among themselves, which may, under severe penalties, prohibit all unlicensed excursions into the upper *world*; though I confess this seems not so...

Thus we see the *Ayr* <note>original: "Ayr"; in this context, it refers to the "aerial" realm where spirits were thought to reside</note> will be *re-peopled* after the *conflagration*: but how the *Earth* will so soon be restored to *Inhabitants*, is a matter of some difficulty to determine since it useth to be furnisht <note>useth to be furnisht: is usually supplied or populated</note> from the *Aerial* regions, which now will have none left that are fit to plant it. <page-num>191</page-num>

# A Key for Providence.

Now whatever becomes of this particular draft <note>original: "draught," meaning a sketch or preliminary version of an idea</note> of the Soul’s various **conditions** of life and action, the main Opinion of **Preexistence** <term>Preexistence: the philosophical and theological belief that the human soul existed before the body was conceived</term> is not at all concerned. This scheme is only to show that natural and imperfect Reason can frame an **Intelligible Idea** <note>A concept that can be grasped or understood by the human mind</note> of it; And therefore without question the Divine **Wisdom** could form and order it, either in this way, or with infinitely more accuracy and exactness. How it was with us in the past, therefore, I do not know; But yet that we may have existed, and acted before we descended into this world, I think is very probable. And I see no reason why **Preexistence** may not be admitted without altering anything significant in the ordinary system of Theology. But I conclude with that modest conclusion of the Great **Descartes** <note>René Descartes (1596–1650), the influential French philosopher. The author is quoting from the conclusion of Descartes' *Principles of Philosophy*.</note>.

That although these matters seem hardly understandable in any other way than as I have here explained them:

Yet nevertheless, remembering that I am not infallible, I assert nothing; But submit all I have written to the Authority—

<meta>catchword: rity</meta>

<summary>The author argues that the preexistence of souls is a logically plausible concept that does not contradict established theology. He concludes by adopting a stance of intellectual humility, quoting René Descartes to express his submission to higher authority.</summary>

<keywords>preexistence of souls, René Descartes, Divine Wisdom, human reason, theological systems, intellectual humility, philosophy of mind</keywords>
